that should normally be present are diminished or lost.\n  manifestation: When I’m not all wound up, I can sit for hours with my coffee getting cold, not answering calls, not going to the walking club, and not even caring to pick up my paintbrush.\n- key: S033\n  name: Elevated_Irritable_Mood\n  description: A distinct period of abnormally and persistently elevated, expansive, or irritable mood.\n  manifestation: I go from feeling so bright and chosen that Greenville can’t hold all my plans to snapping hard at someone over a small thing, like a client asking me to repeat myself.\n- key: S034\n  name: Increased_Activity\n  description: Abnormally and persistently increased goal-directed activity or energy.\n  manifestation: I get so much energy that I’ll clean my whole kitchen, start three paintings, crochet half a blanket, and rewrite care schedules all in the same night without being able to sit down.\n- key: S035\n  name: Inflated_Self_Esteem\n  description: Inflated supposed to build a whole new care-and-art program that doctors and agencies would come learn from me.\n- key: S036\n  name: Decreased_Sleep\n  description: Decreased need for sleep (e.g., feels minute I’m thinking about a client’s medicine, then rent, then a painting title, then some message in a song, and I can’t slow it down enough to make sense.\n- key: S039\n  name: outside, a bird at the window, or a color on the table pulls me away and I’m off doing something else before I know it.\n- key: additional_requirement_1\n  name: Additional Requirement 1\n duration of the active and residual portions of the illness\n  manifestation: For most of this stretch, I was either wound up with big plans, little sleep, and nonstop activity, or coming down from I can’t slow it down enough to make sense or say it right.\n\nDoctor: Do you have days where you feel like you're unable to take care of yourself or your daily routine?\n\nPatient: Yes. I can get where I feel real bright, like I’m chosen or something, like Greenville can’t even hold all the plans I’ve got in my head. And then I can turn around and snap at somebody over nothing, like if a client asks me to repeat myself. I’ll be in my little studio and it feels like somebody is right there watching, even when the house is quiet and I know nobody’s there. It makes me doubt what I’m working on.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find it hard to express your thoughts or have trouble keeping your thoughts straight when these voices are talking to you?\n\nPatient: Yes. When that’s happening, my thoughts get going too fast. Like one minute I’m thinking about a client’s medicine, then I’m worrying about rent, then I’m thinking of a title for a painting, then some message I heard in a song… and it just jumps around. I can’t slow it down enough to make sense or say it right.\n\nDoctor: Do you have days where you feel like you're unable to take care of yourself or your daily routine?\n\nPatient: Yes. Some days I just don’t keep up like I used to. It got bad enough that my aide job was almost in trouble because I missed shifts and mixed up my care notes. I couldn’t keep the visits organized right.\n\nAnd at home, I stopped doing things I used to do, like my walking club and the art talks at the library. I’d mostly just stay by myself at home.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your mood, such as feeling extremely happy or irritable for no reason?\n\nPatient: Yes. I can get where I feel real bright, like I’m chosen or something, like Greenville can’t even hold all the plans I’ve got in my head. And then I can turn around and snap at somebody over nothing, like if a client asks me to repeat myself. It feels bigger than it should.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself saying things that don't make sense to others when you're talking to them?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I know sometimes people look confused when I’m talking, and I might lose my place or not explain it right. But I don’t always know if it actually doesn’t make sense to them or if I just got ahead of myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to stick to one topic when you're talking to someone?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. My thoughts go so fast I can’t stay on one thing. I’ll start talking about a client’s medicine, then suddenly I’m on rent, then a painting title, then something I heard in a song that feels like it means something. It’s like I can’t slow it down enough to make it come out right.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your thoughts are disorganized or hard to understand even when the voices are not present?\n\nPatient: Yes, even when I’m not hearing anything, my thoughts can still be all over the place. They run so fast—like I’ll be thinking about a client’s medicine, then rent, then a painting title, then some message in a song. I can’t slow it down enough to make it come out clear.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you've been feeling less emotional than usual, or that it's harder for you to express your emotions to others?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. When I’m not all wound up, I can just sit there for hours with my coffee getting cold. I won’t answer calls, I don’t go to the walking club, and I don’t even feel like picking up my paintbrush. It’s not that I’m crying or anything… it’s more like I just don’t care or can’t get myself to show much.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I came in because I keep hearing voices. It’s like somebody is right there in my little studio, clear as anything, saying my name and telling me my work is being judged. But the house is quiet, and when I look, nobody’s there.\n\nDoctor: What kind of things are the voices saying about your work and you?\n\nPatient: They say my name, like they’re right there in the room with me. And they keep saying my work is being judged… like somebody is watching over it and deciding if it’s good or bad. It sounds real clear, even when it’s quiet and I know nobody’s there.\n\nDoctor: Are the voices telling you to do anything?\n\nPatient: Not really telling me to do something, no. It’s more like they say my name and keep saying my work is being judged, like somebody’s right there in the studio watching it. But I don’t see anyone.\n\nDoctor: Are the voices you hear all the same voice or are there different voices?\n\nPatient: It seems like the same kind of voice most of the time. Clear, like somebody standing there in my studio, saying my name and talking about my work being judged. I don’t always know who it is, but it sounds real.\n\nDoctor: Do the voices influence what you do or how you feel about yourself or your work?\n\nPatient: Yes… it does. When I hear them saying my name and saying my work is being judged, it makes me feel like I’m not doing good enough. I’ll be in my little studio and it feels like somebody is right there watching, even when the house is quiet and I know nobody’s there. It makes me doubt what I’m working on.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find it hard to express your thoughts or have trouble keeping your thoughts straight when these voices are talking to you?\n\nPatient: Yes. When that’s happening, my thoughts get going too fast. Like one minute I’m thinking about a client’s medicine, then I’m worrying about rent, then I’m thinking of a title for a painting, then some message I heard in a song… and it just jumps around. I can’t slow it down enough to make sense or say it right.\n\nDoctor: Do you have days where you feel like you're unable to take care of yourself or your daily routine?\n\nPatient: Yes. Some days I just don’t keep up like I used to. It got bad enough that my aide job was almost in trouble because I missed shifts and mixed up my care notes. I couldn’t keep the visits organized right.\n\nAnd at home, I stopped doing things I used to do, like my walking club and the art talks at the library. I’d mostly just stay by myself at home.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your mood, such as feeling extremely happy or irritable for no reason?\n\nPatient: Yes. I can get where I feel real bright, like I’m chosen or something, like Greenville can’t even hold all the plans I’ve got in my head. And then I can turn around and snap at somebody over nothing, like if a client asks me to repeat myself. It feels bigger than it should.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself saying things that don't make sense to others when you're talking to them?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I know sometimes people look confused when I’m talking, and I might lose my place or not explain it right. But I don’t always know if it actually doesn’t make sense to them or if I just got ahead of myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to stick to one topic when you're talking to someone?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. My thoughts go so fast I can’t stay on one thing. I’ll start talking about a client’s medicine, then suddenly I’m on rent, then a painting title, then something I heard in a song that feels like it means something. It’s like I can’t slow it down enough to make it come out right.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your thoughts are disorganized or hard to understand even when the voices are not present?\n\nPatient: Yes, even when I’m not hearing anything, my thoughts can still be all over the place. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n15\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF20.0, F23, F25.1\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I came in because I keep hearing voices. It’s like somebody is right there in my little studio, clear as anything, saying my name and telling me my work is being judged. But the house is quiet, and when I look, nobody’s there.\n\nDoctor: What kind of things are the voices saying about your work and you?\n\nPatient: They say my name, like they’re right there in the room with me. And they keep saying my work is being judged… like somebody is watching over it and deciding if it’s good or bad. It sounds real clear, even when it’s quiet and I know nobody’s there.\n\nDoctor: Are the voices telling you to do anything?\n\nPatient: Not really telling me to do something, no. It’s more like they say my name and keep saying my work is being judged, like somebody’s right there in the studio watching it. But I don’t see anyone.\n\nDoctor: Are the voices you hear all the same voice or are there different voices?\n\nPatient: It seems like the same kind of voice most of the time. Clear, like somebody standing there in my studio, saying my name and talking about my work being judged. I don’t always know who it is, but it sounds real.\n\nDoctor: Do the voices influence what you do or how you feel about yourself or your work?\n\nPatient: Yes… it does. When I hear them saying my name and saying my work is being judged, it makes me feel like I’m not doing good enough. I’ll be in my little studio and it feels like somebody is right there watching, even when the house is quiet and I know nobody’s there. It makes me doubt what I’m working on.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find it hard to express your thoughts or have trouble keeping your thoughts straight when these voices are talking to you?\n\nPatient: Yes. When that’s happening, my thoughts get going too fast. Like one minute I’m thinking about a client’s medicine, then I’m worrying about rent, then I’m thinking of a title for a painting, then some message I heard in a song… and it just jumps around. I can’t slow it down enough to make sense or say it right.\n\nDoctor: Do you have days where you feel like you're unable to take care of yourself or your daily routine?\n\nPatient: Yes. Some days I just don’t keep up like I used to. It got bad enough that my aide job was almost in trouble because I missed shifts and mixed up my care notes. I couldn’t keep the visits organized right.\n\nAnd at home, I stopped doing things I used to do, like my walking club and the art talks at the library. I’d mostly just stay by myself at home.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your mood, such as feeling extremely happy or irritable for no reason?\n\nPatient: Yes. I can get where I feel real bright, like I’m chosen or something, like Greenville can’t even hold all the plans I’ve got in my head. And then I can turn around and snap at somebody over nothing, like if a client asks me to repeat myself. It feels bigger than it should.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself saying things that don't make sense to others when you're talking to them?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I know sometimes people look confused when I’m talking, and I might lose my place or not explain it right. But I don’t always know if it actually doesn’t make sense to them or if I just got ahead of myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to stick to one topic when you're talking to someone?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. My thoughts go so fast I can’t stay on one thing. I’ll start talking about a client’s medicine, then suddenly I’m on rent, then a painting title, then something I heard in a song that feels like it means something. It’s like I can’t slow it down enough to make it come out right.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your thoughts are disorganized or hard to understand even when the voices are not present?\n\nPatient: Yes, even when I’m not hearing anything, my thoughts can still be all over the place. They run so fast—like I’ll be thinking about a client’s medicine, then rent, then a painting title, then some message in a song. I can’t slow it down enough to make it come out clear.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you've been feeling less emotional than usual, or that it's harder for you to express your emotions to others?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. It’s not that I’m crying or anything… it’s more like I just don’t care or can’t get myself to show much.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt extremely sad or depressed for most of the day?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call it extremely sad. Like they’re trying to steal some chance that was meant for me. Even when people tell me that can’t be true, it still feels real to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the belief that your work is being judged by others is something that you can question or doubt when you're not hearing the voices?\n\nPatient: Not really. Even when the voices aren’t going on right then, I still get it in my head that the other home-care agency, and some people from the library, are watching my paintings and my client papers.     "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n16\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF20.0, F23, F25.1\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I came in because I keep hearing voices. It’s like somebody is right there in my little studio, clear as anything, saying my name and telling me my work is being judged. But the house is quiet, and when I look, nobody’s there.\n\nDoctor: What kind of things are the voices saying about your work and you?\n\nPatient: They say my name, like they’re right there in the room with me. And they keep saying my work is being judged… like somebody is watching over it and deciding if it’s good or bad. It sounds real clear, even when it’s quiet and I know nobody’s there.\n\nDoctor: Are the voices telling you to do anything?\n\nPatient: Not really telling me to do something, no. It’s more like they say my name and keep saying my work is being judged, like somebody’s right there in the studio watching it. But I don’t see anyone.\n\nDoctor: Are the voices you hear all the same voice or are there different voices?\n\nPatient: It seems like the same kind of voice most of the time. Clear, like somebody standing there in my studio, saying my name and talking about my work being judged. I don’t always know who it is, but it sounds real.\n\nDoctor: Do the voices influence what you do or how you feel about yourself or your work?\n\nPatient: Yes… it does. When I hear them saying my name and saying my work is being judged, it makes me feel like I’m not doing good enough. I’ll be in my little studio and it feels like somebody is right there watching, even when the house is quiet and I know nobody’s there. It makes me doubt what I’m working on.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find it hard to express your thoughts or have trouble keeping your thoughts straight when these voices are talking to you?\n\nPatient: Yes. When that’s happening, my thoughts get going too fast. Like one minute I’m thinking about a client’s medicine, then I’m worrying about rent, then I’m thinking of a title for a painting, then some message I heard in a song… and it just jumps around. I can’t slow it down enough to make sense or say it right.\n\nDoctor: Do you have days where you feel like you're unable to take care of yourself or your daily routine?\n\nPatient: Yes. Some days I just don’t keep up like I used to. It got bad enough that my aide job was almost in trouble because I missed shifts and mixed up my care notes. I couldn’t keep the visits organized right.\n\nAnd at home, I stopped doing things I used to do, like my walking club and the art talks at the library. I’d mostly just stay by myself at home.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your mood, such as feeling extremely happy or irritable for no reason?\n\nPatient: Yes. I can get where I feel real bright, like I’m chosen or something, like Greenville can’t even hold all the plans I’ve got in my head. And then I can turn around and snap at somebody over nothing, like if a client asks me to repeat myself. It feels bigger than it should.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself saying things that don't make sense to others when you're talking to them?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I know sometimes people look confused when I’m talking, and I might lose my place or not explain it right. But I don’t always know if it actually doesn’t make sense to them or if I just got ahead of myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to stick to one topic when you're talking to someone?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. My thoughts go so fast I can’t stay on one thing. I’ll start talking about a client’s medicine, then suddenly I’m on rent, then a painting title, then something I heard in a song that feels like it means something. It’s like I can’t slow it down enough to make it come out right.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your thoughts are disorganized or hard to understand even when the voices are not present?\n\nPatient: Yes, even when I’m not hearing anything, my thoughts can still be all over the place. They run so fast—like I’ll be thinking about a client’s medicine, then rent, then a painting title, then some message in a song. I can’t slow it down enough to make it come out clear.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you've been feeling less emotional than usual, or that it's harder for you to express your emotions to others?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. When I’m not all wound up, I can just sit there for hours with my coffee getting cold. I won’t answer calls, I don’t go to the walking club, and I don’t even feel like picking up my paintbrush. It’s not that I’m crying or anything… it’s more like I just don’t care or can’t get myself to show much.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt extremely sad or depressed for most of the day?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call it extremely sad. I get low sometimes, and kind of shut down, but I don’t know about depressed most of the day like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that the voices you're hearing are real or that the things they say about your work being judged are actually true?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do believe it when it’s happening. I know people say it doesn’t make sense, but I get it in my head that the other home-care agency, and even some people from the library, are watching my paintings and my client papers. Like they’re trying to steal some chance that was meant for me. Even when people tell me that can’t be true, it still feels real to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the belief that your work is being judged by others is something that you can question or doubt when you're not hearing the voices?\n\nPatient: Not really. Even when the voices aren’t going on right then, I still get it in my head that the other home-care agency, and some people from the library, are watching my paintings and my client papers. People tell me it doesn’t make sense, but I still feel like I know they’re doing it… like they’re trying to steal some chance that was meant for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that other people can actually hear these voices or know what they're saying about your work?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if other people can hear them. But when the house is quiet and nobody’s there, I’m the one hearing it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Clear, like somebody standing there in my studio, saying my name and talking about my work being judged. When I hear them saying my name and saying my work is being judged, it makes me feel like I’m not doing good enough. I’ll be in my little studio and it feels like somebody is right there watching, even when the house is quiet and I know nobody’s there. It makes me doubt what I’m working on.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find it hard to express your thoughts or have trouble keeping your thoughts straight when these voices are talking to you?\n\nPatient: Yes. When that’s happening, my thoughts get going too fast. Like one minute I’m thinking about a client’s medicine, then I’m worrying about rent, then I’m thinking of a title for a painting, then some message I heard in a song… and it just jumps around. I can’t slow it down enough to make sense or say it right.\n\nDoctor: Do you have days where you feel like you're unable to take care of yourself or your daily routine?\n\nPatient: Yes. I couldn’t keep the visits organized right.\n\nAnd at home, I stopped doing things I used to do, like my walking club and the art talks at the library. I’d mostly just stay by myself at home.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your mood, such as feeling extremely happy or irritable for no reason?\n\nPatient: Yes. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I came in because I keep hearing voices. It’s like somebody is right there in my little studio, clear as anything, saying my name and telling me my work is being judged. But the house is quiet, and when I look, nobody’s there.\n\nDoctor: What kind of things are the voices saying about your work and you?\n\nPatient: They say my name, like they’re right there in the room with me. And they keep saying my work is being judged… like somebody is watching over it and deciding if it’s good or bad. It sounds real clear, even when it’s quiet and I know nobody’s there.\n\nDoctor: Are the voices telling you to do anything?\n\nPatient: Not really telling me to do something, no. It’s more like they say my name and keep saying my work is being judged, like somebody’s right there in the studio watching it. But I don’t see anyone.\n\nDoctor: Are the voices you hear all the same voice or are there different voices?\n\nPatient: It seems like the same kind of voice most of the time. Clear, like somebody standing there in my studio, saying my name and talking about my work being judged. I don’t always know who it is, but it sounds real.\n\nDoctor: Do the voices influence what you do or how you feel about yourself or your work?\n\nPatient: Yes… it does. When I hear them saying my name and saying my work is being judged, it makes me feel like I’m not doing good enough. I’ll be in my little studio and it feels like somebody is right there watching, even when the house is quiet and I know nobody’s there. It makes me doubt what I’m working on.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find it hard to express your thoughts or have trouble keeping your thoughts straight when these voices are talking to you?\n\nPatient: Yes. When that’s happening, my thoughts get going too fast. Like one minute I’m thinking about a client’s medicine, then I’m worrying about rent, then I’m thinking of a title for a painting, then some message I heard in a song… and it just jumps around. I can’t slow it down enough to make sense or say it right.\n\nDoctor: Do you have days where you feel like you're unable to take care of yourself or your daily routine?\n\nPatient: Yes. Some days I just don’t keep up like I used to. It got bad enough that my aide job was almost in trouble because I missed shifts and mixed up my care notes. I couldn’t keep the visits organized right.\n\nAnd at home, I stopped doing things I used to do, like my walking club and the art talks at the library. I’d mostly just stay by myself at home.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your mood, such as feeling extremely happy or irritable for no reason?\n\nPatient: Yes. I can get where I feel real bright, like I’m chosen or something, like Greenville can’t even hold all the plans I’ve got in my head. And then I can turn around and snap at somebody over nothing, like if a client asks me to repeat myself. It feels bigger than it should.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself saying things that don't make sense to others when you're talking to them?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I know sometimes people look confused when I’m talking, and I might lose my place or not explain it right. But I don’t always know if it actually doesn’t make sense to them or if I just got ahead of myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to stick to one topic when you're talking to someone?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. My thoughts go so fast I can’t stay on one thing. I’ll start talking about a client’s medicine, then suddenly I’m on rent, then a painting title, then something I heard in a song that feels like it means something. It’s like I can’t slow it down enough to make it come out right.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your thoughts are disorganized or hard to understand even when the voices are not present?\n\nPatient: Yes, even when I’m not hearing anything, my thoughts can still be all over the place. They run so fast—like I’ll be thinking about a client’s medicine, then rent, then a painting title, then some message in a song. I can’t slow it down enough to make it come out clear.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you've been feeling less emotional than usual, or that it's harder for you to express your emotions to others?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. When I’m not all wound up, I can just sit there for hours with my coffee getting cold. I won’t answer calls, I don’t go to the walking club, and I don’t even feel like picking up my paintbrush. It’s not that I’m crying or anything… it’s more like I just don’t care or can’t get myself to show much.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt extremely sad or depressed for most of the day?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call it extremely sad. I get low sometimes, and kind of shut down, but I don’t know about depressed most of the day like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that the voices you're hearing are real or that the things they say about your work being judged are actually true?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do believe it when it’s happening. I know people say it doesn’t make sense, but I get it in my head that the other home-care agency, and even some people from the library, are watching my paintings and my client papers. Like they’re trying to steal some chance that was meant for me. Even when people tell me that can’t be true, it still feels real to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the belief that your work is being judged by others is something that you can question or doubt when you're not hearing the voices?\n\nPatient: Not really. It’s like somebody is right there in my little studio, clear as anything, saying my name and telling me my work is being judged. And they keep saying my work is being judged… like somebody is watching over it and deciding if it’s good or bad. It sounds real clear, even when it’s quiet and I know nobody’s there.\n\nDoctor: Are the voices telling you to do anything?\n\nPatient: Not really telling me to do something, no. It’s more like they say my name and keep saying my work is being judged, like somebody’s right there in the studio watching it. But I don’t see anyone.\n\nDoctor: Are the voices you hear all the same voice or are there different voices?\n\nPatient: It seems like the same kind of voice most of the time. Clear, like somebody standing there in my studio, saying my name and talking about my work being judged. I don’t always know who it is, but it sounds real.\n\nDoctor: Do the voices influence what you do or how you feel about yourself or your work?\n\nPatient: Yes… it does. When I hear them saying my name and saying my work is being judged, it makes me feel like I’m not doing good enough. I’ll be in my little studio and it feels like somebody is right there watching, even when the house is quiet and I know nobody’s there. It makes me doubt what I’m working on.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find it hard to express your thoughts or have trouble keeping your thoughts straight when these voices are talking to you?\n\nPatient: Yes. When that’s happening, my thoughts get going too fast. Like one minute I’m thinking about a client’s medicine, then I’m worrying about rent, then I’m thinking of a title for a painting, then some message I heard in a song… and it just jumps around. I can’t slow it down enough to make sense or say it right.\n\nDoctor: Do you have days where you feel like you're unable to take care of yourself or your daily routine?\n\nPatient: Yes. Some days I just don’t keep up like I used to. It got bad enough that my aide job was almost in trouble because I missed shifts and mixed up my care notes. I couldn’t keep the visits organized right.\n\nAnd at home, I stopped doing things I used to do, like my walking club and the art talks at the library. I’d mostly just stay by myself at home.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your mood, such as feeling extremely happy or irritable for no reason?\n\nPatient: Yes. I can get where I feel real bright, like I’m chosen or something, like Greenville can’t even hold all the plans I’ve got in my head. And then I can turn around and snap at somebody over nothing, like if a client asks me to repeat myself. It feels bigger than it should.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself saying things that don't make sense to others when you're talking to them?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I know sometimes people look confused when I’m talking, and I might lose my place or not explain it right. But I don’t always know if it actually doesn’t make sense to them or if I just got ahead of myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to stick to one topic when you're talking to someone?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. My thoughts go so fast I can’t stay on one thing. I’ll start talking about a client’s medicine, then suddenly I’m on rent, then a painting title, then something I heard in a song that feels like it means something. It’s like I can’t slow it down enough to make it come out right.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your thoughts are disorganized or hard to understand even when the voices are not present?\n\nPatient: Yes, even when I’m not hearing anything, my thoughts can still be all over the place. They run so fast—like I’ll be thinking about a client’s medicine, then rent, then a painting title, then some message in a song. I can’t slow it down enough to make it come out clear.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you've been feeling less emotional than usual, or that it's harder for you to express your emotions to others?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. It’s not that I’m crying or anything… it’s more like I just don’t care or can’t get myself to show much.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt extremely sad or depressed for most of the day?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call it extremely sad. I get low sometimes, and kind of shut down, but I don’t know about depressed most of the day like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that the voices you're hearing are real or that the things they say about your work being judged are actually true?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do believe it when it’s happening. I know people say it doesn’t make sense, but I get it in my head that the other home-care agency, and even some people from the library, are watching my paintings and my client papers. Like they’re trying to steal some chance that was meant for me. Even when people tell me that can’t be true, it still feels real to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the belief that your work is being judged by others is something that you can question or doubt when you're not hearing the voices?\n\nPatient: Not really. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n20\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF20.0, F23, F25.1\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I came in because I keep hearing voices. It’s like somebody is right there in my little studio, clear as anything, saying my name and telling me my work is being judged. But the house is quiet, and when I look, nobody’s there.\n\nDoctor: What kind of things are the voices saying about your work and you?\n\nPatient: They say my name, like they’re right there in the room with me. And they keep saying my work is being judged… like somebody is watching over it and deciding if it’s good or bad. It sounds real clear, even when it’s quiet and I know nobody’s there.\n\nDoctor: Are the voices telling you to do anything?\n\nPatient: Not really telling me to do something, no. It’s more like they say my name and keep saying my work is being judged, like somebody’s right there in the studio watching it. But I don’t see anyone.\n\nDoctor: Are the voices you hear all the same voice or are there different voices?\n\nPatient: It seems like the same kind of voice most of the time. Clear, like somebody standing there in my studio, saying my name and talking about my work being judged. I don’t always know who it is, but it sounds real.\n\nDoctor: Do the voices influence what you do or how you feel about yourself or your work?\n\nPatient: Yes… it does. When I hear them saying my name and saying my work is being judged, it makes me feel like I’m not doing good enough. I’ll be in my little studio and it feels like somebody is right there watching, even when the house is quiet and I know nobody’s there. It makes me doubt what I’m working on.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find it hard to express your thoughts or have trouble keeping your thoughts straight when these voices are talking to you?\n\nPatient: Yes. When that’s happening, my thoughts get going too fast. Like one minute I’m thinking about a client’s medicine, then I’m worrying about rent, then I’m thinking of a title for a painting, then some message I heard in a song… and it just jumps around. I can’t slow it down enough to make sense or say it right.\n\nDoctor: Do you have days where you feel like you're unable to take care of yourself or your daily routine?\n\nPatient: Yes. Some days I just don’t keep up like I used to. It got bad enough that my aide job was almost in trouble because I missed shifts and mixed up my care notes. I couldn’t keep the visits organized right.\n\nAnd at home, I stopped doing things I used to do, like my walking club and the art talks at the library. I’d mostly just stay by myself at home.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your mood, such as feeling extremely happy or irritable for no reason?\n\nPatient: Yes. I can get where I feel real bright, like I’m chosen or something, like Greenville can’t even hold all the plans I’ve got in my head. And then I can turn around and snap at somebody over nothing, like if a client asks me to repeat myself. It feels bigger than it should.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself saying things that don't make sense to others when you're talking to them?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I know sometimes people look confused when I’m talking, and I might lose my place or not explain it right. But I don’t always know if it actually doesn’t make sense to them or if I just got ahead of myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to stick to one topic when you're talking to someone?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. My thoughts go so fast I can’t stay on one thing. I’ll start talking about a client’s medicine, then suddenly I’m on rent, then a painting title, then something I heard in a song that feels like it means something. It’s like I can’t slow it down enough to make it come out right.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your thoughts are disorganized or hard to understand even when the voices are not present?\n\nPatient: Yes, even when I’m not hearing anything, my thoughts can still be all over the place. They run so fast—like I’ll be thinking about a client’s medicine, then rent, then a painting title, then some message in a song. I can’t slow it down enough to make it come out clear.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you've been feeling less emotional than usual, or that it's harder for you to express your emotions to others?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. When I’m not all wound up, I can just sit there for hours with my coffee getting cold. I won’t answer calls, I don’t go to the walking club, and I don’t even feel like picking up my paintbrush. It’s not that I’m crying or anything… it’s more like I just don’t care or can’t get myself to show much.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt extremely sad or depressed for most of the day?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call it extremely sad. I get low sometimes, and kind of shut down, but I don’t know about depressed most of the day like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that the voices you're hearing are real or that the things they say about your work being judged are actually true?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do believe it when it’s happening. I know people say it doesn’t make sense, but I get it in my head that the other home-care agency, and even some people from the library, are watching my paintings and my client papers. Like they’re trying to steal some chance that was meant for me. Even when people tell me that can’t be true, it still feels real to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the belief that your work is being judged by others is something that you can question or doubt when you're not hearing the voices?\n\nPatient: Not really. Even when the voices aren’t going on right then, I still get it in my head that the other home-care agency, and some people from the library, are watching my paintings and my client papers. People tell me it doesn’t make sense, but I still feel like I know they’re doing it… like they’re trying to steal some chance that was meant for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that other people can actually hear these voices or know what they're saying about your work?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if other people can hear them. To me they sound real clear, like somebody’s standing right there in my little studio, saying my name and talking about my work being judged. But when the house is quiet and nobody’s there, I’m the one hearing it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that the belief in your work being judged by others is something that could be false, even if it feels real to you?\n\nPatient: I mean… people tell me it could be false, but I don’t really feel that way. I get it in my head that the other home-care agency and some people from the library are watching my paintings and my client papers, like they’re trying to steal a chance that was meant for me. Since then, the voices and that feeling like people are judging my work have been going on.\n\nDoctor: Do you have periods where the voices and the feeling of being judged go away completely?\n\nPatient: No, not completely. Even when people tell me that doesn’t make sense, I still feel like I know they’re doing it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
